--- Page 19 ---
<language>Latin</language>
<page-type>illustration</page-type>
<page-num>2</page-num>
<meta>ms.sup.A.48. Arts</meta>

<image-desc>
A vertical arrangement of three rectangular miniatures illustrating medieval lore concerning the lion, set against a parchment background.

1. **Top Miniature:** A tan-colored lion with a prominent mane and a long, whip-like tail is shown in profile biting the neck of a small, dark-furred ape or monkey. The scene is set against a blue background decorated with a white grid pattern, framed by gold and green bands and an outer red border.

2. **Middle Miniature:** A large, orange-red lion with bared teeth dominates the left side of the frame. Below its paws, two men lie prostrate on the ground in a submissive posture. Behind the lion and the prostrate men, three figures—two men and a woman wearing a wimple—are walking away, carrying bundles suspended from poles over their shoulders. The background is dark blue with white scrolling vine patterns. The frame consists of blue and white bands with an outer red border. This scene likely depicts the belief that lions spare those who prostrate themselves before them.

3. **Bottom Miniature:** A tan lion is depicted with its head and front quarters lowered toward the ground in a submissive or fearful stance. To the left, a white rooster with a red comb and wattle is perched on a small stylized branch, facing the lion. The background is blue with small white crosses or florets. The frame is gold and green with an outer red border. This illustrates the traditional belief that the crowing of a cock terrifies a lion.
</image-desc>

<vocab>Leo, Simia, Gallus, prostratio, bestiarium, illumination, manuscript</vocab>

--- Page 20 ---
<language>Latin</language>
<page-type>illustration</page-type>

<image-desc>
A manuscript page featuring three horizontal, rectangular miniatures depicting scenes involving lions, framed by red, gold, and green borders. 

1. **Top Register**: A large, stylized orange-red lion sits on a wavy yellow ground. Behind the lion is a decorative tree with a green trunk and blue, fan-like foliage. The background is a vibrant blue within a gold inner frame.
2. **Middle Register**: A pale tan lion is depicted lying down. Three small animals—a blue-grey one, a yellowish one, and a reddish one—are perched on the lion's back. The background consists of a gold rectangle set against a blue field.
3. **Bottom Register**: Two adult lions, one tan and one orange-red, lean over a small reddish cub that lies on its back. This scene illustrates the medieval bestiary legend that lion cubs are born dead and are brought to life on the third day by the breath of their father.
</image-desc>

<vocab>Leo, lion, bestiary, miniature, illumination</vocab>

<image-desc>An illuminated miniature at the bottom of the page, enclosed in a red and blue rectangular frame. It shows a series of three pits or vessels set into a stylized landscape of colorful, overlapping hillocks in shades of blue, green, and yellow.
- On the left, two small golden-brown animals (leontophones) are curled inside a pit.
- In the center, a lion has fallen head-first into a pit, with only its hindquarters and tail visible.
- On the right, a small white animal stands on a green rectangular grate or platform placed over a pit that appears to contain fire, depicted with red patterns.
This sequence illustrates the traditional bestiary account of how the leontophone is captured and burned so that its ashes can be used to poison lions.</image-desc>

--- Page 28 ---
<language>Latin</language>
<page-type>illustration</page-type>

<image-desc>
A full-page medieval illumination divided into two horizontal panels within a broad red frame.

The upper panel depicts the bestiary legend of the tiger and the mirror. A large, blue-furred beast with faint spots (the tiger) stands over a circular, red-rimmed mirror on the ground. The animal is bowed low, gazing at its own reflection visible within the glass. To the right, a large stylized tree with multiple branches ending in artichoke-like buds grows upward. The background is divided between polished gold and a red field decorated with a white lattice pattern.

The lower panel shows a hunter dressed in a red tunic and a green cloak, riding a white horse at a gallop. He holds a raised sword in his right hand and appears to be carrying a small tiger cub against his chest with his left arm. He looks back toward the scene above. <image-desc>A rectangular manuscript illumination depicting a Bonnacon, a legendary bull-like creature, in a landscape. The beast is colored reddish-orange with horns that curve inward toward its forehead. It is positioned on the right side of the scene, turning its head back toward its pursuers. Three figures are hunting it: in the foreground, a man in a blue and red tunic thrusts a long spear at the animal; behind him is a smaller figure in an orange tunic; and to the left, another man in a brownish tunic raises an axe. The Bonnacon is shown expelling a stream of fire or caustic dung from its rear towards the hunters, a defense mechanism described in medieval bestiaries. The background consists of stylized green trees and a vibrant blue field decorated with a pattern of white concentric circles. The miniature is framed by a border of red, green, and white lines.</image-desc>

<image-desc>
A framed square miniature with a decorative red and blue border. The background of the scene consists of a central gold leaf square surrounded by a reddish-brown field patterned with small gold crescents and dots. The illustration depicts five badgers (taxus) engaged in cooperative labor. One badger lies on its back (supine) in the center, acting as a living sledge; it holds a large pile of brown earth on its belly with all four paws and grips a wooden stick horizontally in its mouth. Four other grey-blue badgers, two on each side, grip the ends of the stick with their teeth and pull the central badger backwards to transport the excavated soil.
</image-desc>

A medieval manuscript illumination depicting the Cinnomolgus bird. A stylized, symmetrical tree with a light green trunk and branches ending in trilobed leaves of various colors (blue, green, orange, and purple) grows in the center of a decorated frame. At the top of the tree, two greyish-brown birds sit in a nest made of twigs. Below the tree, two men look up at the birds. The man on the left wears a bi-colored orange and green tunic and points toward the nest; the man on the right wears a purple-brown tunic and holds a curved object, likely a sling or a tool. The background is divided into quadrants of blue and red, with a central gold leaf panel behind the upper portion of the tree. The miniature is bordered by a simple multi-colored frame. Below the image is a block of Latin text with a large, ornate blue and red initial 'C'.
</image-desc>

<image-desc>A rectangular illuminated miniature with a thick red border containing a gold inner frame. The background is a deep blue, decorated with a regular pattern of small white and gold quatrefoil flowers. In the center, a large owl (Bubo) is depicted in shades of brown and tan, facing forward with large, detailed eyes. It perches on a stylized branch with green and blue foliage. Two smaller birds are present: one on the left in flight toward the owl, and another on the right, resembling a magpie, perched on a branch and looking back at the owl. Vipera dicta quod ui pariat. Nam cum uenter eius ad partum matuerit. catuli non expectantes naturam datam solutionemque rosis lateribus eius erumpunt in uicem matris interitu. Fertur autem quod masculus ore inserto uipere semen expuat. Illa autem ex uoluptate in rabiem uersa caput maris ore receptum praedicet ita fit ut parens uterque pereat. masculus dum coit femina cum parit. Denique dicit sanctus ambrosius quod nequissimum genus bestie est et super omnia que serpentium sunt generis astutior ubi coeundi cupiditatem assumpserit: murene maritime notam sibi requirit copulam uel yparat. Progressaque ad litora sibilo testificata presenciam suū ad coniugalem amplexum illam euocat. Murena autem inuitata non deest et uenenate serpenti expetitos usus sue impertit coniunctionis. Quid igitur sermo huiusmodi uult nisi ferendos esse mores coniugum et si absens est ei compienda presencia.

To the left, there is a grey four-legged animal resembling an otter or beaver and a red sea-dog with a canine head and fish tail. To the right is an orange creature with a long, swan-like neck. <image-desc>A full-page medieval illumination within a decorative red, white, and blue border against a gold leaf background. The scene depicts the legendary "Aspidochelone" or giant sea monster/whale mistaken for an island. 

In the center, a wooden clinker-built ship with a single mast and a furled white sail is anchored. The ship features a "forecastled" structure at the stern where two figures are visible; one in a red hood looks out from the top, and another steers with a long oar from below. A third figure in a green tunic stands on the deck near the mast. A fourth figure, wearing a blue tunic and a white coif, is shown mid-air or climbing the rigging.

Below the ship, a man in a red tunic is on what appears to be a small island but is actually the back of a massive sea creature. He is tending to a cooking pot or cauldron over a fire built directly on the creature's back. 

The creature (the Aspidochelone) occupies the bottom of the frame, depicted with large scales, a prominent eye, and a mouth filled with sharp teeth, partially submerged in stylized green and blue waves. Small tufts of greenery grow on its back, further reinforcing the illusion of land. 

Outside the frame to the right, there are faint sketches of a hand holding what appears to be a leash or tether, possibly related to the neighboring page's content.</image-desc>

<vocab>Aspidochelone, whale, ship, mariners, illumination, Bestiary, sea monster</vocab>
